CARRY-OVER LEGISLATION
1999 ANNUAL COMMUNICATION

Grand Master Nahin’s Recommendation No. 1
Repeals Inspectors Tenure

The wisdom and experience of all who are a part of our Grand Lodge
are very important. We should, at all times, endeavor to strengthen our
Jurisdiction. A prudent approach would and should be to build and benefit from
our strengths and address our weaknesses. This appertains not only to
procedures and established practices but to individuals as well. A major
concern, within our structure, is that of the position of Inspector. This appointed
position requires that the brother not only be duly qualified but also devote an
enormous amount of personal time. Much talent exists within our Jurisdiction,
but experience proves that talent alone does not produce a desirable Inspector.
An effective Inspector must commit himself to a great amount of personal time
and dedication. Many are willing to serve as an Inspector, but few are willing or
able to commit the personal dedication and time that is needed. I believe that
tenure of an Inspector should be based upon a periodic evaluation to determine
the need for change or for reappointment. Change is definitely necessary, from
time to time, and should be made as required. Those that demonstrate wisdom,
talent and commitment of time should be given serious consideration for
reappointment, regardless of time served. Time and experience have produced
the most effective Inspectors. Often times, in our challenges of today, it is very
difficult to find individuals with these desired qualities, especially the
commitment of personal time, which is so necessary.

I believe that the tenure of an Inspector’s appointment or reappointment
be at the discretion of the Grand Master and that the nine-year limitation should
be discontinued.

If you adopt this recommendation, Section 4010 of the California
Masonic Code will be amended to read as follows:

§4010. APPOINTMENT.

The Grand Master shall divide the Jurisdiction into districts, and shall appoint
for each district an Inspector, who shall be a Past Master of this Jurisdiction, and
who shall, before entering upon his duties, produce a certificate from the Grand
Lecturer or an Assistant Grand Lecturer that he is proficient in the work and
lectures adopted by the Grand Lodge and is qualified to act as Inspector. Each
Inspector shall serve for a term of one year, commencing on January 1 following
the Grand Master’s installation and terminating on December 31 of the same
year.


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 237

 

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

Grand Master Nahin’s Recommendation No. 1 seeks to remove the
nine (9) year limit on the term of Inspectors.

Section 4010 of the California Masonic Code currently allows the
Grand Master to reappoint an Inspector for the good of the Craft.

The established policy was created to permit more leadership
participation within the Grand Lodge.

If approved, this could possibly create an inconsistency within the
Grand Lodge, as committeemen would still be limited to nine (9) years.

This Recommendation is not consistent with Grand Lodge policy and
general purposes.

Recommendation NOT ADOPTED.

Grand Master Nahin’s Recommendation No. 5
Implements a Candidate Education Program

It is abundantly clear to any of us who sit in this room as active
members of the Masonic Fraternity and particularly of the Grand Lodge of
California that sufficient attention has not been and is not now being paid to the
education of our candidates for the Entered Apprentice, Fellowcraft or Master
Mason Degrees. We know that such education is necessary, or rather that it is
vital if we are to maintain our position in the State as a premiere fraternal
organization. We cannot just make Masons by conferring the rituals or having a
ritualistic proficiency of whatever kind or description. Men who become
members of our Craft must know and must understand what it is they are
joining, what has happened to our fraternity before and what may happen to our
fraternity in the future. They can do that only by having education.

Recent modifications of the candidate proficiency requirements have
resulted in elimination of any requirement other than memorization of a part of
the proficiency catechism, resulting in newly-made Masons who haven’t been
informed of Masonic history or Masonic philosophy or Masonic law.

Masonic information for candidates is essential and desirable because:

1. Each candidate for Freemasonry should be knowledgeable as
to its romantic history and beginnings, its high purpose and ideals, the meaning


238 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

of its ritualistic symbolism, its ethics and philosophy and the application of all
these aspects to everyday living and the improvement of man;

2. One of the purposes of Freemasonry is to make its members
wiser as well as happier and better;

3. Such information will enable each candidate to conform to its
requirements and avoid the things prohibited by it;

4. The teachings of the Fellow Craft Degree inform us that we
are to attain knowledge through diligence and industry; and

bb Our public relations would be enhanced as our members
become better able to intelligently discuss what Freemasonry is and what it does
for its members, their families, the community and the nation.

It is therefore my recommendation that we add to the proficiency
requirements for all three Degrees a “book test” by requiring each candidate to
read the applicable pamphlet of the Basic Masonic Education Course pertaining
to the Degree he is seeking to be proficient in and to answer in writing the
questions which relate to that pamphlet in “open book” fashion. These
questions shall be prepared by the Grand Lodge Education Committee in
consultation with the Grand Lodge Ritual Committee. The answers of each
candidate are to be approved by a Lodge committee consisting of three elected
officers or three Past Masters or a combination thereof and the answers to be
retained as a part of the Lodge records for a period of not less than one year.

I further recommend that the Committee on Masonic Education prepare
a clearly written procedure with instructions and sample answers to be
distributed to each Lodge. This will assure consistent implementation of this
addition to the candidate proficiencies and appropriate monitoring of its use and
value. (They shall also provide that these pamphlets and questions and the
procedure and sample answers be made available on the Internet.)

If you adopt this recommendation, Section 26340 of the California
Masonic Code will be amended to read as follows:

§26340. ADVANCEMENT.
A Lodge shall advance an Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft to a higher degree if

A. Hhe has given satisfactory evidence, by examination in open Lodge, that

he is proficient and well qualified in the degrees which he has already
taken. To demonstrate such proficiency, an Entered Apprentice or Fellow
Craft shall only be required to recite from memory the obligation of each
degree, together with the signs and modes of recognition of each degree.
Any number of applicants may be examined at a meeting, but not more
than five shall be examined at any one time with the questions being asked


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 239

in rotation. Such examination shall be held in front of the East between
the altar and the Master’s station.

He has completed the Basic Masonic Education Course in the degrees
which he has already taken He shall demonstrate such completion by
answering in writing and _in open book fashion the questions
appertaining to each of the pamphlets of the Basic Masonic Education
Course, as such questions and pamphlets may be promulgated from
time to time by the Grand Master for this purpose. The candidate’s
written answers shall be approved by a committee of three elective
officers or three Past Masters or a combination thereof and shall be

retained as a part of the Lodge records for a period of not less than one

‘ear.

|p

 

If for any reason there is an erroneous conferral of a degree or degrees, the
candidate retains the status reached but must wait one year and present an
application for a higher degree or degrees.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

Past Grand Master Nahin, a member of this committee, did not participate
in the preparation of this report.

Past Grand Master Nahin is correct in his observation that there has been
little effort expended in educating our new candidates with regard to the history,
philosophy, and relevancy of Masonry. This situation was further aggravated by
the reduction in the proficiency requirement. While Section 6160 of our Masonic
Code charges the Education Committee to promote Masonic education, this effort
is largely directed at lodges or other groups, not at individuals.

It is Most Worshipful Nahin’s proposal that we require Masonic education
for all new Masons while still avoiding rote memorization. There is precedent for
written examinations. Sections 25070C, 25080C and 25090C of the California
Masonic Code mandate that incoming Masters and Wardens be proficient with the
Masonic Code; open-book, written examinations have been employed to fulfill that
requirement for many years.

It is not the intent of Masonry simply to have members pay dues and go to
social events. Masonry espouses a value system that is often neglected in our
current society. The dissemination, support, and practice of our value system are
the things that differentiate us from other social or service organizations. A
promulgation of the Masonic value system is essential to society as a whole, and we
need to conduct it whenever and wherever we can. An operrbook examination will
allow the candidate to learn about Masonry, including its values, and to discuss this
material freely with his family and friends. The process may even produce more
enthusiastic participation by the candidate and increased numbers of membership
requests from his acquaintances.


240 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The proposal is consistent with our current policies and purposes.

Report of the Committee on Ritual:

The Ritual Committee heartily concurs with Grand Master Nahin’s
Recommendation No. 5 and recommends a “yes” vote.

Recommendation ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 99-07
Permits a Non-masonic Stenographic Reporter
At a Masonic Trial

The proponents of this Resolution seek to permit a non-Mason to act as a
stenographic reporter at a Masonic trial at the discretion of the Trial Master if no
disclosure of Masonic secrets would occur.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, Section 32290 of the California Masonic Code provides
that the Trial Master, in a trial of an individual Mason, may employ a duly
accredited stenographic reporter to transcribe the proceedings of such trial; and

WHEREAS, Section 32290 also requires that any such stenographic
reporter be a Master Mason; and

WHEREAS, within the State of California, very few, if any, duly
accredited stenographic reporters are Master Masons; and

WHEREAS, it is frequently beneficial to have a verbatim stenographic
record of the trial of an individual Mason; and

WHEREAS, most trials of individual Masons do not involve disclosure
of any of the secrets of Freemasonry; and
WHEREAS, it would be beneficial to permit a Trial Master the option

and discretion to have a stenographic reporter transcribe the proceedings if a
reporter who is a Master Mason is not available.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 32290 of the
California Masonic Code is amended as marked:

§32290. STENOGRAPHIC REPORTER.

At the option of the Trial Master, a duly accredited stenographic
reporter may be employed if-he—is—a—Master—Masen. Whenever
reasonably possible, such reporter shall be a Master Mason. The Trial


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 241

Master shall, however, have the option to employ a_stenographic
reporter who is not a Master Mason if he has first reasonable concluded

that_the conduct of the proceedings is not likely to involve the
disclosure of any of the secrets of Freemasonry. If the reporter is a
Master Mason, hHe shall qualify upon his honor as a Master Mason
before entering upon his duty. The expenses of such reporter shall be
borne by the Lodge of which the accused is a member, if it is in this
Jurisdiction.

 

When the accused is not a member in this Jurisdiction, Grand Lodge
shall pay the expenses of the reporter if:

A. The reporter is employed with the approval of the Grand
Master;

B. The claim of the reporter is forwarded to the Grand Secretary;
and

Cc. The claim is certified as correct by the Master and Secretary of

the Lodge in which the trial was held.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

Our Masonic Code currently permits the Trial Master in a Masonic
Trial to employ a duly accredited stenographic reporter to report the Trial if the
reporter is a Master Mason.

The proponents of this Resolution seek to permit a non-Mason to serve
as the stenographic reporter at a Masonic Trial at the discretion of the Trial
Master if no disclosure of Masonic secrets would occur.

Since accuracy and fairness of the complete trial record is always of
primary importance in a Masonic Trial, this proposed Resolution is consistent
with current Grand Lodge policy and general purposes.

Report of the Committee on Trial Review:

Your Committee on Trial Review has on many occasions been faced
with the difficult task of attempting to evaluate trial proceedings without a clear
and accurate record. Audio taped proceedings, although helpful, generally are
of poor quality and difficult to understand. An accurate written transcript
provided by a qualified court reporter would be very helpful in allowing your
committee to accurately and efficiently evaluate trial records with a higher level
of confidence that we have received a full, complete and accurate record.

Passage of this resolution would not require the use of a court reporter
in all trials. However, in those trials of more complicated matters a written


242 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

transcript would assist the work of your committee to ensure that Masonic
justice is done.

§15165. GRAND MASTER’S YOUTH SUPPORT AWARD.

Order of the Rainbow for Girls, DeMolay International’s Jurisdiction of
Northern California _and_DeMolay_International’s Jurisdiction of

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

Resolution No. 99-09 codifies the Grand Master=s Youth Support
Award as an official award.

The proponents of this Resolution seek to legislate what the Grand
Master is already doing.

Adding legislation to cover a practice that is already being done is not
consistent with Grand Lodge policy and general purposes.

Resolution No. 99-11
Permits Appointment of Assistant Lodge Secretary
and Allows Compensation

The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow individual Lodges the option of
creating the office of Assistant Secretary.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, the consolidation of Lodges has resulted in an increased
burden on many of the Lodge Secretaries; and

WHEREAS, the loss of a Secretary during his period of service, either
through illness, death or resignation, has caused severe problems in maintaining
the proper continuity required in the successful operation of Lodge activities;
and

WHEREAS, the addition of an Assistant Secretary would benefit many
Lodges in preparing and training a replacement for the current Secretary; and

WHEREAS, the creation of such an office would be at the discretion
and vote of the Lodge members.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Article VI of the
Uniform Code of By-laws in Section 19040 of the California Masonic Code be
amended as marked:

ARTICLE VI
THE SECRETARY

Section 1. The Secretary shall:

A. Keep a faithful record of all proceedings proper to be written;
B. Transmit a copy of the same to Grand Lodge when required;
Cc. Keep a separate account for each member of the Lodge, and

for report at the Stated Meetings in August and February of
each year, for the six month period ending on the preceding
June 30 and the 12 month period ending on the preceding
December 31 respectively, the amounts due by each;

D. Receive all monies due the Lodge, and pay the same monthly
or more frequently as necessary, to the Treasurer; or shall
deposit the same not less frequently than monthly to the credit
of the Treasurer of the Lodge, in some bank or trust company
designated by the Lodge, taking duplicate deposit tags or
receipts therefor, one to be immediately delivered to the


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 245

Treasurer, the other to be kept by the Secretary as a voucher;
and

E. Perform such other duties as may properly pertain to his
office.

Section 2. He shall receive such compensation for his services as the
Lodge may direct.

Section 3. If the office of Assistant Secretary is authorized by the
Lodge, that officer shall assist the Secretary in the performance of all of
his duties. The Assistant Secretary shall receive such compensation for
his services as the Lodge may direct,

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

Resolution No. 99-11 codifies the position of Assistant Secretary and
allows for compensation for that position. Under Article I, Section 1 of the
Uniform Code of By-Laws of Lodges, the Lodge has the authority to appoint an
officer whenever it deems that position to be appropriate. Since some of these
officers are already compensated, it is consistent with our current policy to
extend this practice to an Assistant Secretary.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

The proponents are seeking to eliminate possible confusion over
multiple resolutions on the same subject matter. They also wish to mandate the
priority for consideration and to limit presentation of failed resolutions to five
years from the last communication at which they were presented.

It is the policy of this Grand Lodge to encourage participation by the
Brothers in the legislative process of their Grand Lodge.

The delegates should be able to study, participate in discussions and
use their best judgment in voting.

To limit participation in the legislative process is not consistent with
the policy and general purposes of Grand Lodge.

252 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

 

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

The proponents of the Resolution seek to correct outdated references
and clarify language in the California Masonic Code relating to Lodge
investments. In general, such a change is appropriate and consistent with our
policies and purposes. However, several of the changes that are being proposed
do raise some technical issues.

A requirement has been added that mandates the Lodge to confer and
counsel with a “Registered Investment Advisor” for any proposed investments.
There are numerous recognized Investment Advisors that are legally permitted
to provide investment advice. This Resolution limits Lodge investment advice
to a “Registered Investment Advisor” only. The utilization of such an individual
may result in increased costs to the Lodge, if the Lodge previously relied on
investment advice from some other recognized advisor, such as the present
Lodge Investment Program.

Next, the requirement that a Registered Investment Advisor shall be
used by the Lodge may lead to a potential conflict of interest. This is especially
the case if the advisor consulted by the Lodge recommends the purchase of
certain investments and then sells those recommended investments to the Lodge.

Lastly, the language of the Resolution changes the word “funds” to
“assets.” This latter term is more inclusive than the former; assets are likely to
represent a larger total sum than funds, as the value of such things as property
would be included.

It is not the policy of Grand Lodge to require Lodges to undertake extra
expenses nor to confer exclusive rights to provide investment advice to one
select group.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 99-18
Requires Return of Dues Receipt Before Dues Paid in Advance Are
Refunded Upon Dimit or Discontinuation of Multiple Membership

The proponents of this Resolution seek to require the return of the dues receipt
before dues are refunded upon a dimit or discontinuance of multiple
memberships.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 253

WHEREAS, Section 27080 of the California Masonic Code allows a
member to keep in his possession his paid up dues receipt; and

WHEREAS, upon granting a dimit the Brother may continue to attend
Lodge for the balance of the year.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 27080 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§27080. RETURN OF DUES.

If a brother dies, is issued a Dimit or discontinues multiple
membership, the Lodge shall refund to the proper estate or to him, dues
paid in advance of the current month when he died or his membership
terminated; provided, however, that_a brother issued _a Dimit or
discontinuing multiple membership may only obtain a refund of his
dues paid in advance if he has returned his dues receipt in such Lodge
with his request _for_a Dimit_or Certificate of Discontinuance of
Multiple Membership.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

Resolution No. 99-18 addresses the issue of a dimit or discontinuance
of a multiple membership. If a member chooses to end his relationship with our
fraternity or a particular Lodge, and requests a refund of unused dues, returning
his dues receipt would be in order.

The Resolution as presented is consistent with Grand Lodge policy and
general purposes.

Resolution No. 99-23
Permits Solicitation of Support for the Hiram Foundation

The proponents of this Resolution seek to permit solicitation of California
Masonic support for the Hiram Foundation Incorporated.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, the Hiram Foundation Incorporated was formed on July
20, 1998 as a California nonprofit public benefit corporation by a group of
concerned Masons to provide emergency disaster relief to Masons and their
families;


254 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

WHEREAS, the need for the program of emergency relief in case of
disaster has been established;

WHEREAS, while the Red Cross may be the first agency on the scene
of a disaster but its support is quite limited and the Federal Emergency
Management Agency (FEMA) is mainly concerned with rebuilding, the support
of the Hiram Foundation Incorporated is directed toward filling the gap between
the Red Cross and FEMA to supplement other sources of supply for needed
essentials;

WHEREAS, it is not anticipated that the Hiram Foundation
Incorporated would provide medical assistance or major items of long term
shelter and transportation but may assist in making arrangements for same;

WHEREAS, individual Lodges have the responsibility to assist their
members in time of need but are not equipped, organized or financed to handle
emergencies resulting from disasters;

WHEREAS, the Hiram Foundation Incorporated has been registered in
the State of California as a self-funded charitable corporation with a Board of
Directors and Officers who stand ready to implement the plans; and

WHEREAS, in order for the Hiram Foundation Incorporated to solicit
support of California Lodges and Masons for funds to support disaster relief,
Grand Lodge of California recognition is required.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that a new Section 28060 be
added to the California Masonic Code to read as follows:

§28060. HIRAM FOUNDATION,

The Grand Lodge of California recognizes the Hiram Foundation
Incorporated, a California nonprofit public benefit corporation, formed
on July 20, 1998, as a self-funded charity operated for the purpose of
providing emergency disaster relief to Masons and their families. No
Grand Lodge funds may be used to support the operations of the Hiram
Foundation Incorporated. However, Lodges of this Jurisdiction and
their members may be solicited for support of those programs of the
Hiram Foundation Incorporated which provide emergency disaster
relief to Masons and their families. Such relief may given to members
of Lodges of this Jurisdiction and_ members of Lodges under Grand
Lodges recognized by the Grand Lodge of California who reside _in

California. Relief may also be given to California members residing in
other jurisdictions. Lodges are authorized to receive applications for

such relief.

For purposes of this program, a disaster is defined as any interruption in
normal living conditions due to accident or natural causes suchas fire,
flood, earthquake, hurricane, tornado, acts of violence or terrorism or


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 255

any other similar event. It is not necessary to wait for a declaration of
disaster by a governmental agency in order to render assistance.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

About two years ago, a California Mason became aware of the plight of
another Mason who had suffered from a disaster and needed immediate support
to get him through the first few weeks. Unfortunately, no relief was so available
from a variety of Masonic sources. Although that problem was ultimately
resolved, this California Mason realized that there was no avenue for the prompt
provision of housing, clothing, or other necessities to individual Masons who
suffer calamitous loss. In order to make such relief available, a charitable
corporation was established, the Hiram Foundation, Incorporated. However, in
order for this Foundation to solicit financial support from California Masons and
Lodges, it must have official Grand Lodge recognition. The proponents are thus
presenting this legislation.

Charity is a cornerstone of Masonry and, on that basis, this resolution
does have merit. However, if this were to become law, there would be a very
important distinction between the relationship of the Hiram Foundation and
Grand Lodge and the one that currently exists between Grand Lodge and the
Masonic Homes, Masonic Foundation, and Masonic Temple Association. By
recognizing the Hiram Foundation in our Masonic Code, we assume
responsibility for its actions and could even be held accountable for any default
or liability that that body assumes. However, the Hiram Foundation is not
accountable to Grand Lodge at the time when it takes its actions. For example,
Grand Lodge has no power to appoint representatives to the Hiram Foundation
Board.

To assume the potential liability for any organization that is not
accountable to this Grand Lodge is not consistent with our policies and general
purposes.

Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 99-31
Reduces Age for Membership From 21 to 18

The proponents of this Resolution seek to reduce from 21 to 18 the age
requirement for admission into Freemasonry within the Grand Jurisdiction of
California.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:


256 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

WHEREAS, California Masonry has upheld the idea that the minimum
age requirement for membership in a Blue Lodge is 21;

WHEREAS, the consequence of this idea, or code, is that it leaves a 3
year gap between being a legal adult in the real world and becoming qualified to
be a member of our fraternity;

WHEREAS, California will recognize a brother under the age of 21 as
a member if he is raised from another jurisdiction which California recognizes
with all the rights and privileges; and

WHEREAS, this penalizes California men, from ages 18 to 21, who
want to become a member but can’t simply because they just happen to live in
California.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 26010 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§26010. MORAL AND PHYSICAL QUALIFICATIONS.
An applicant for the degrees of Masonry must be:

A. A man who has attained the age of 24 18 years;

B A believer in a Supreme Being and a future existence;

iC Of good moral conduct; and

D Without physical or mental impairment that would prevent

him from learning and practicing the principles of Masonry or
which would create a burden on the Lodge.

The question as to the physical or mental acceptability of the applicant
shall be left to the sound discretion of the Lodge. The Master shall
state the nature and extent of any such impairment upon receiving the
application and before the ballot is taken.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 26090 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§26090. APPLICATIONS.

Applications for the degrees and for affiliation shall be on the form
approved by Grand Lodge, signed by the applicant, or for affiliation, by
mark properly attested, if unable to write by reason of physical
affliction, and recommended by at least two members of the Lodge. A
recommender need not have previously known the applicant, as long as
prior to signing the application he first makes a careful investigation to


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 257

ascertain if the applicant is worthy. Recommendation of an applicant is
a personal pledge of Masonic honor to the Lodge that the applicant
possesses the positive qualifications required. Any recommendation
once given cannot be withdrawn after the application has been received
by the Lodge. References need not be Masons or of the male gender.

Applications for degrees shall not be received unless the portion which
is annexed to, and part of, the form prescribed has been answered. An
applicant may sign the completed application by signature or by a mark
attested to by two members of the Lodge. An application is received
when it is read by the Secretary at a Stated Meeting, unless, by a
majority vote, the Lodge declines to receive it, which is equivalent to
its withdrawal.

The Lodge cannot receive an application until the applicant has attained
the age of 24 18 years. The Master may not refuse to receive an
application or direct the Secretary to withhold it for any other reason.

The refusal of a Lodge to receive an application is equivalent to its
withdrawal, and the applicant need not wait 12 months before
presenting another application to that or any other Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

The proponents of this Resolution seek to reduce from 21 to 18 the age
requirement for admission into Freemasonry within the Grand Jurisdiction of
California.

Section 26010 A of the California Masonic Code now states that “An
applicant for the degrees of Masonry must be a man who has attained the age of
21 years.”

This Resolution has been presented to Grand Lodge on several
occasions. The past discussions, while providing speculative opinions, have not
presented any facts or data to support either side of the argument. It is difficult
to formulate policy in the absence of such information. Since our age
requirement is 21, this Resolution is not consistent with our current policy.

Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 99-32
Permits Mountain Shade Lodge No. 18, Gravel Range Lodge No. 59 and
Harmony Lodge No. 164 to Hold Only Ten Stated Meetings Per Year

The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow Gravel Range Lodge No. 59,
Mountain Shade Lodge No. 18 and Harmony Lodge No. 164 to hold only ten
Stated meetings a year.


258 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, the above named Lodges have very small memberships
from which to attract enough officers and members to provide a quorum for
their stated meetings;

WHEREAS, these Lodges are located in the higher snow laden areas of
the Sierra Nevada Mountain Range of California, making transportation often
difficult; and

WHEREAS, the membership of these Lodges are all older persons and
perhaps more infirm to this inclement weather;

WHEREAS, the town population has declined in the past 75 years from
two to five thousand people in the Gold Rush era to the present population of
250 to 500; and

WHEREAS, over one half of the membership of these Lodges lives 30
to 100 miles distant from their meeting places, and the excessive travel during
inclement weather and the traffic conditions make transportation a threat rather
than a pleasure for these older members to attend.

WHEREAS, it is known that other Lodges under the jurisdiction of the
Grand Lodge of the State of California are located in the desert areas of our
State and the hot meeting months make attendance of these dwindling, elderly
members more of a burden than a joy to attend their stated meetings.

§16030. STATED MEETINGS.

A Lodge shall hold a Stated Meeting for the transaction of its
regular business once in each calendar month, except that
Lodges whose By-Laws provide for meetings fixed by the
phase of the moon shall hold a Stated Meeting each lunar
month. The foregoing notwithstanding, Gravel Range Lodge
No. 59, Mountain Shade Lodge No. 18 and Harmony Lodge
No. 164 shall only be required to hold a Stated Meeting for the
transaction of its regular business once in each of ten calendar
months during the year, as it may provide in its By-laws.

A Lodge may be opened for a Stated Meeting prior to the time
fixed in its By-Laws. No business shall be transacted prior to
such time.

The Inspector may grant a dispensation to change the date of a
Stated Meeting, as provided for elsewhere in this Code.

In the event that the Master of a Lodge determines that
inclement weather, including ice, snow, and flood conditions,
existing on the day established in the By-Laws of the Lodge


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 259

for its Stated Meeting, makes it hazardous for members of the
Lodge to attend the meeting, he may, with the consent of the
Inspector of the District to which the Lodge is assigned,
postpone the Stated Meeting to the same day of the week next
following the week scheduled for the Stated Meeting. Upon
such postponing, the Master shall notify, to the extent
possible, all members of the Lodge residing within 50 miles of
the meeting place of the Lodge.

Should a Stated Meeting be postponed, the business transacted
at the postponed Stated Meeting shall be limited to the reading
of the minutes of the last Stated and subsequent Special
Meetings, action thereon, payment of the invoices of the
Lodge, and such other business of an emergent nature as
determined by the Master to require immediate consideration.
No other new business shall be considered.

California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

The proponents of Resolution No. 99-32 seek to permit Mountain
Shade Lodge No. 18, Gravel Range Lodge No. 59 and Harmony Lodge No. 164
to hold only ten Stated Meetings per year.

The concerns of the proponents about the age and welfare of their
members are shared by several Lodges in this jurisdiction. Section 16030 of the
California Masonic Code provides for a change of the date and time of a Stated
Meeting when weather conditions create a hazard.

This Resolution creates several potential problems:

First, it will disrupt the Lodges system of paying bills on a monthly
basis.

Second, the selection of which months to meet would be left to the
discretion of the individual Lodges. This could be addressed with by-law
changes but could also result in requests for Special Dispensations for election
of officers and additional Resolutions.

Third, balloting on a petition for degrees or affiliation could be delayed
for several months.

Perhaps most importantly, this Resolution will discourage the Lodges
from meeting on a monthly basis. Grand Lodge has a long standing policy that
“business” should not be the sole activity at Stated Meetings, but that the
activities should include dinners, programs, socialization, fellowship and
Masonic education.

This Resolution is not consistent with Grand Lodge policy or general
purposes.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 00-01
Changes Application Forms

The proponents of this Resolution seek to change the procedure for modifying
the Application for Degrees and Application for Affiliation forms.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, any changes in the standardized forms of the application
for the degrees and for affiliation now in use must be approved by the Grand
Lodge; and

WHEREAS, this requirement makes the application for the degrees and
for affiliation inflexible and difficult to adapt to contemporary methods of
distribution and communication; and

WHEREAS, it would be helpful to include certain new questions on the
application forms, such as “wife’s name” and “DeMolay membership and
experience,” as well as removing certain other questions that are no longer
applicable.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 26090 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§26090. APPLICATIONS.

Applications for the degrees and for affiliation shall be on the
form approved by Grand-Ledge-the Grand Master, signed by
the applicant, or for affiliation, by mark properly attested, if
unable to write by reason of physical affliction, and
recommended by at least two members of the Lodge. A
recommender need not have previously known the applicant,
as long as prior to signing the application he first makes a
careful investigation to ascertain if the applicant is worthy.
Recommendation of an applicant is a personal pledge of
Masonic honor to the Lodge that the applicant possesses the
positive qualifications required. Any recommendation once
given cannot be withdrawn after the application has been
received by the Lodge. References need not be Masons or of
the male gender.

 


262

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

application by signature or by a mark attested to by two
members of the Lodge. An application is received when it is
read by the Secretary at a Stated Meeting, unless, by a
majority vote, the Lodge declines to receive it, which is
equivalent to its withdrawal.

The Lodge cannot receive an application until the applicant
has attained the age of 21 years. The Master may not refuse to
receive an application or direct the Secretary to withhold it for
any other reason.

The refusal of a Lodge to receive an application is equivalent
to its withdrawal, and the applicant need not wait 12 months
before presenting another application to that or any other
Lodge.

Resolution No. 00-02
Changes Committee Sizes

The proponents of this Resolution seek to grant the Grand Master flexibility in
determining the size of standing committees of Grand Lodge.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, the per capita tax is based on the budgetary needs of
Grand Lodge and a portion of the Grand Lodge Budget is used to fund the
committees of Grand Lodge; and

WHEREAS, the membership of Grand Lodge continues to decline, and
from year to year the amount of work needed to be done by Grand Lodge
committees can vary, yet the size of some of these committees is fixed in the
California Masonic Code; and

WHEREAS, each Grand Master should have the ability to adjust the
size of all Grand Lodge committees to match the changing needs of our craft and
our budget, yet the California Masonic Code does not give the Grand Master
any flexibility about the size of the standing committees of Grand Lodge.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 6020 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

The proponents of this Resolution seek to grant the Grand Master
flexibility in determining the size of Standing Committees of Grand Lodge.

Research into our Masonic history going back to the late 1800’s
suggests that one of the most commonly proposed resolutions is that creating
and changing the size of a committee of the Grand Lodge. This requires the
usual type of Resolution, referral to committee, discussion before the Grand
Lodge and finally a vote of the Grand Lodge in order to give effect to the change
in the size or type of committee of the Grand Lodge.

Ross Digest, the 1943 Edition of our Code, had 20 different changes
merely to create or change the number of members of various Grand Lodge
standing committees. The 1975 Red Book has five.

This creates a significant use of Grand Lodge time in performing what
should be a ministerial duty.

Our code states very succinctly at Section 6020 referring to Standing
Committees, “The members of Standing Committees shall be appointed by the
newly installed Grand Master at each Annual Communication before the close
thereof.” That is simple enough.

It is the prerogative and the responsibility of the Grand Master to
appoint his Standing committees. The power to appoint also creates the inherent
power to change the numbers of the members of the standing committees were it
not for the past history of our Grand Lodge which specifically reflects the
appointment of a certain number of members for each committee.


266 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

For example Jurisprudence, Policy & General Purposes (this
committee) and Investments have nine members each. That is not to say we
need nine members or that we need more but merely the statement of the law is
that the Grand Master must appoint nine members of each of those three
committees, no more B no less.

Section 350 of the California Masonic Code states as follows: “The
Grand Master, during the interval between the Annual Communications of
Grand Lodge, possesses all of its executive powers, except the granting of
charters.”

This means inherently the creating of a committee or appointment of
members to the various standing committees under his jurisdiction. It would
seem good business practice as well as the more efficient conduct of Grand
Lodge activities to enable the Grand Master the flexibility to determine how
many members of each standing committee there should be. This Resolution is
consistent with the policy and general purposes of the Grand Lodge as expressed
in defining the executive authority of the Grand Master.

Resolution ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 00-03
Changes Dates of Annual Lodge Elections and Installation of Officers

The proponents of this Resolution seek to change the Annual Election of
Officers to the September Stated Meeting and the Installation of Officers to the
period between the close of the Annual Communication and the third week in
November.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, at this time in our history as a Nation, we as Men and
Masons find ourselves pressed with more and more demands on our time; and

WHEREAS, this increased demand erodes the quality and quantity of
time available for our enjoyment of families, relatives and friends; and

WHEREAS, the current policy of the Grand Lodge of California,
concerning Elections and Installations, places these important events in constant
competition with some of our most cherished and important holidays.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 25010 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 267

§25010. ELECTION.

The election of the Master, Wardens, Treasurer and Secretary
of each Lodge shall be by ballot. The Lodge, by unanimous
consent, may permit any designated officer to cast the ballot
for the Lodge. If an eligible candidate fails to receive a
majority of the votes cast, and the candidate receiving a
majority is ineligible, there is no election. A blank ballot shall
not constitute a vote cast. A majority of all votes cast shall be
necessary for an election. An annual election held contrary to
the provision of the section shall be void.

The Master, Wardens, Treasurer and Secretary of each Lodge
shall be elected annually at the Stated Meeting in Nevember
September each year.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 25120 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§25120. INSTALLATION.

The officers, including incumbents, shall be installed in the
manner prescribed by Grand Lodge, as soon as practical after

their—election—or—appointment the close _of the Annual
Communication of the Grand Lodge and before the fourth
week of November, if practical, and shall hold their
respective offices until their successors have been duly elected
or appointed and installed. Officers elected or appointed to fill
vacancies between the annual installations of the Lodge shall
be installed in the manner prescribed by Grand Lodge, as soon

as practical after their election or appointment.

The installation may be performed by the Master or, by
invitation of the Master, by any qualified Past Master of any
jurisdiction. It is not the privilege of the Master-elect to select
the Installing Officer or in any way interfere with the duties
and privileges of the office of Master. If performed by a Past
Master, the Master or a Warden of the Lodge must be present.
There can be no installation by proxy. Installation may be
private or public. Two Lodges may participate in a joint
installation.

The Certificates required for installation, endorsed by the
Installing Officer, shall be forwarded to the Grand Secretary.


268 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

The proponents seek to change the dates of the annual election of lodge
officers to the September stated meeting and the installation of lodge officers to
a period of time fixed between the close of Grand Lodge and before the fourth
week of November.

Currently, elections are held in November and installations are to be
held as soon as practical thereafter. The actual dates for installations are
determined by mutual agreement among all the lodges and concordant and
appendant bodies which share the Masonic facility. Many installations are held
as late as December and even January.

To allow even more flexibility in scheduling, installations are permitted
on Sundays. Additionally, some organizations, like Order of Eastern Star, have
their installation dates specified (in November) by statute.

To now require that installations be held within such a restrictive period
of time could create scheduling nightmares for all organizations involved.

This resolution is not consistent with Grand Lodge Policy or General
Purposes.

Report of the Committee on Ritual:

This resolution is a matter of policy and not ritual. However, the
adoption of this resolution would place an added burden on Officers for
qualification. We recommend a “no” vote.

Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 00-04
Combines Dimit and Transfer Forms Into One

The proponents of this Resolution seek to combine Form No. 221, “Dimit of
Master Mason” and Form No. 225, “Discontinuance of Multiple Membership”
into one form, “Withdrawal of Membership.”

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, when a member desires to transfer membership from one
Lodge to become a member of another, it is advisable that he become elected to
the second Lodge (i.e. multiple member) before terminating his membership in
the first lodge; and


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 269

WHEREAS, the Lodge from which the member is withdrawing does
not always know the intent of a member when affiliating with another Lodge;
and

WHEREAS, Section 23020C of the California Masonic Code states
that “A member may .. . affiliate with any one or more other Lodges under this
Grand Lodge . . . He becomes a plural member.”; and

WHEREAS, Section 23050 of the California Masonic Code states,
“Upon notification of election to membership in another lodge, [the Secretary]
shall, upon request, issue a dimit,”; and

WHEREAS, Form No. 221 (Rev 86), “Dimit of Master Mason” states,
“This document is not to be issued for termination of Multiple Membership”;
and

WHEREAS, the only forms available for termination of membership
are either “Dimit” or “Discontinuance of Plural Membership,” and there is no
provision for a “Transfer” of membership; and

WHEREAS, the Monthly Report has only one section, “Withdrawal of
Member” to cover Dimit, Transfer and Discontinuance; and

WHEREAS, to eliminate the confusion of semantics, the following
changes are recommended.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Grand Secretary
issue a single form by which a member may request an open dimit, indicate to
his Lodge his intent to transfer his membership to another Lodge or request a
discontinuance of multiple membership in the Lodge.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Grand Secretary issue a single
form by which the Secretary of a Lodge may certify that a member has been
granted an open dimit, a member’s membership in the Lodge has terminated
because he has transferred his membership to another Lodge or a member’s
multiple membership in the Lodge has ceased.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that that Section 23050 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§23050. DIMITTING.

A member, in good standing, against whom no charges are
pending, may withdraw at any time. Such withdrawal may be
obtained by requesting in writing, over his signature, a Dimit
at a Stated Meeting. Upon request, the Master of the Lodge
shall order the Secretary to issue a Dimit immediately, in the
form provided, as evidence of the withdrawal.


270

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Where transfer of membership from one Lodge to another
within this Jurisdiction is involved, the Secretary, upon receipt
of notification of election to membership, shall immediately
issue the Dimit on the form provided by the Grand Secretary
and report the fact of the issuance to the Lodge at the next
Stated Meeting. The Dimit shall be effective on the first day
of the calendar month following receipt_of notification of
election to membership.

Where transfer of membership from one Lodge to a Lodge in
another Jurisdiction is involved, the Secretary, upon the
request of a member in good standing, may issue a Certificate
of Standing. It shall specify the date through which the
member’s dues are paid and that a Dimit will be issued upon
receipt of the notification of election in another Lodge if such
notification is received prior to the date through which such
dues are paid. On receipt of notification of election, the
Secretary shall immediately issue a Dimit on the form
provided by the Grand Secretary and shall report the fact of
the issuance to the Lodge at the next Stated Meeting. The
Dimit shall be effective on the first day of the calendar month
following receipt of notification of election to membership.

An Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft who has permanently
removed from this Jurisdiction, against whom no charges are
pending, may withdraw from his Lodge at any time by giving
written notification of his intention to do so at a Stated
Meeting, and he may receive a Dimit.

The date of the issuance of a Dimit shall be the date of
termination of membership.

A duplicate Dimit may be issued if the original is lost.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

Resolution 00-04 seeks to require the Grand Secretary to issue a single
form to be used for the issuance of a dimit, the issuance of a dimit with the
intention of transferring membership to another lodge, and the discontinuation
of multiple membership where membership is being retained in one or more

other lodges.

While it may be desirable to combine or amend some forms to simplify
procedures, the present policy of leaving these administrative details in the

hands of the Grand Secretary has sound reasons for being retained.

This resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes of

shall _be issued_a permanent life membership certificate and
shall not receive an annual paid dues receipt.

dues card with no expiration date?

would be difficult, if not impossible, to retrieve. It therefore is not consistent

Resolution No. 00-06
Modifies Rules for Lodge Investments

The proponents of this Resolution seek to conform California Masonic Code
Section 21030 regarding Lodge investments to Section 7020 regarding Grand
Lodge investments.


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 273

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, Section 7020 of the California Masonic Code permits the

WHEREAS, Section 21030 of the California Masonic Code should
permit the Constituent Lodes to invest their funds in a like manner.

California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§21030. INVESTMENT.

A Lodge may invest its funds, exclusive of Lodge Temple
property and Lodge Temple securities, as follows:

A. Such funds shall be invested with the care, skill

prudence and diligence under the circumstances then
prevailing, including but_not limited to the general
economic conditions and the anticipated needs of the
Lodge, that a prudent person acting in a like capacity
and_familiar_with such matters would use in the
conduct of an enterprise of like character and with
like aims to accomplish the purposes of the Lodge.

 

B. The specific _and_ detailed guidelines to govern the
day-to-day investment activities of the Lodge shall be
promulgated from time to time by the Lodge’s
investment committee _or trustees, subject_to the
approval of the Lodge.

 


274 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

 


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 275

 

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

The proponents of this Resolution seek to conform California Masonic
Code Sections 21030 regarding Lodge investments to Section 7020 regarding
Grand Lodge investments.

The criteria for Lodge investments, under current law, is 20% in Cash
or Bank Accounts, 20% in Preferred Stocks, and 60% in Common Stocks,
among other requirements. Furthermore, carry-over Resolution No. 99-15
stipulates that 20% would be in Cash, 80% in stocks.

Resolution No. 00-06 would eliminate any established criteria for
investment and state that “the day-to-day investment activities of the Lodge
shall be promulgated from time to time by the Lodge=s investment committee or
trustees, subject to the approval of the Lodge.”

The Grand Lodge investments under Section 7020 are promulgated
from time to time by a standing committee, the Grand Lodge Committee on
Investments, in consultation with the Grand Treasurer.

This Resolution does not mandate that each Lodge will have a standing
Investment Committee nor Lodge Trustees.


276 PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Notwithstanding the use of prudence in Lodge investments, and the
current economic climate, this resolution provides no specific process in the
protection of the funds. Considering the difference in the management of
investments, between the Grand Lodge and the Lodges and the potential for loss
of financial stability of some Lodges, this Resolution is not consistent with
Grand Lodge Policy and General Purposes.

Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 00-07
Permits All Past Masters to Vote at Grand Lodge

The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow all Past Masters of chartered
Lodges, registered and present during Annual Communication, to vote.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, Section 230 of the California Masonic Code clearly states
that Past Masters of this Jurisdiction are members of Grand Lodge; and

WHEREAS, Section 240 implies that as long as a Past Master
maintains his membership in a Lodge in this Jurisdiction, he is a member of
Grand Lodge; and

WHEREAS, Section 440 precludes representation by proxy; and

WHEREAS, membership in a Fraternal Order habitually entitles that
member a vote on all matters brought to such body; and

WHEREAS, most other Fraternal Grand Lodges encourage their Grand
Lodge members (Past Executives, ie; Past Master, etc.) to attend their Annual
Sessions and participate in the proceedings; and

WHEREAS, Section 620 D is not in keeping with the intent and
purposes of our Constitution, i.e. Right of determination by its members
(violation of Section 230), and violation of its own regulation (Section 440 —
Past Masters collectively of each Chartered Lodge is in actuality a proxy vote),
discourages attendance at the Annual Communication by its members (Past
Masters), and does not promote the most important tenets of our Order.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 620 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:


GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA 277

§620. VOTES.

The following shall each be entitled to one vote:

A. Elective and appointive Grand Lodge Officers except the
Grand Tiler and the Assistant Grand Tiler;

B. Past Grand Officers;

Cc. Masters, Senior Wardens and Junior Wardens of chartered
Lodges; and

D. Past Masters eeHeetively of each chartered Lodges: registered
and_present during the debate on questions on the Grand
Lodge floor.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

Proposed Resolution No. 00-07 would permit any Past Master of a
chartered Lodge who attends the Annual Communication to have an individual
vote on issues brought before Grand Lodge in the same manner as that now
afforded Grand Lodge Officers, Past Grand Officers and the three senior officers
of the constituent Lodges.

The voting rights of Grand Lodge Members, as set forth in California
Masonic Code Section 620, are primarily vested in the constituent Lodges, by
giving each Lodge four votes, one each for the Master, Senior and Junior
Wardens, and a collective vote for the Past Masters of the Lodge. Thus the vast
majority of voting power is spread evenly among the Lodges, and in particular,
in those men who are currently active in the governance of their Lodge.

To give each Past Master a separate vote would contravene Grand
Lodge policy in a number of respects. It would potentially give the older
Lodges (those with a greater number of Past Masters), more voting power than
other Lodges. Next, it would give the Past Masters of any Lodge, men who are
not actively engaged in the day to day governance of their Lodge, greater voting
power than those who run the Lodge on a daily basis. Historically, many if not
more Past Masters attend the Annual Communication as do the senior Lodge
officers, and Past Masters living closer to Grand Lodge are, because of the
expense involved, more likely to attend the Annual Communication than those
living further away, giving these men even greater advantage. Thus, at any
given Annual Communication Past Masters would have the majority of actual
voting power, destroying the balance of power embodied in the Masonic Code.
If the Past Masters of each Lodge retain their single collective vote, the balance
of voting power will remain equitable with all of the constituent Blue Lodges.

This Resolution is not consistent with Grand Lodge Policy.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 00-08
Permits Automatic Qualification of Past Masters

The proponents of this Resolution seek to eliminate the need for Past Masters to
re-qualify in the lectures of the three degrees when holding the offices of
Master, Senior Warden or Junior Warden.

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:

WHEREAS, all Masters of this jurisdiction must have qualified to give
the lecture in all three degrees prior to taking office as Master of their respective
Lodges; and

WHEREAS, many Past Masters refrain from taking a leadership role in
their respective Lodges because of the personal difficulty of relearning the
necessary rituals to requalify; and

WHEREAS, there is a growing need for experienced leadership in our
Masonic Lodges; and

WHEREAS, the most qualified individuals to lead our Masonic Lodges
are those who have direct experience in serving as Masters of Masonic Lodges;
and

WHEREAS, there is a distinct difference between leading a Lodge and
the ability to deliver ritualistic lectures.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 25070 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

§25070. QUALIFICATIONS OF MASTER.

Before a Master-elect can be installed there shall be presented
to the Installing Officer the Certificate of the Grand Lecturer,
Assistant Grand Lecturer of the division in which his Lodge is
situated, or the Inspector of the district in which his Lodge is
situated, certifying that:

A. He has personally examined the Master-elect and that
he is qualified to give the work and lectures in the

and

relate to the government of a Lodge.
